<a id='question-150'></a>

### Question 150: Metabolic Genetics & Clinical Nutrition
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Zellweger syndrome (a peroxisomal biogenesis disorder) is caused by mutations in *PEX* genes, leading to impaired peroxisome formation and function, resulting in accumulation of very long-chain fatty acids (VLCFAs) and specific clinical features. (Zellweger 综合征是一种过氧化物酶体生物合成障碍，由 *PEX* 基因突变引起，导致过氧化物酶体形成和功能受损，从而积累非常长链脂肪酸 (VLCFA) 并产生特定临床特征。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-month-old male infant presents to the pediatric emergency department with lethargy, poor feeding, and hypotonia. He was born full-term after an uncomplicated pregnancy. His parents report that he has always had a high-pitched cry, seizures, and distinctive facial features including a prominent forehead, flattened facial profile, and epicanthal folds. Physical examination reveals hepatomegaly, jaundice, and bilateral cryptorchidism. Neurological examination shows severe developmental delay and absent deep tendon reflexes. Laboratory studies show elevated liver enzymes (AST 350 U/L, ALT 400 U/L), elevated creatine kinase (CK 1200 U/L), and markedly elevated levels of very long-chain fatty acids (VLCFAs) in plasma (C26:0 = 1.2 μmol/L, reference < 0.15 μmol/L). A skin biopsy reveals abnormally small peroxisomes with an increased number of them, consistent with impaired peroxisome biogenesis. Genetic testing confirms a homozygous mutation in the *PEX1* gene.

Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect in this patient's condition?

(A) Deficiency of acyl-CoA oxidase, leading to impaired beta-oxidation of VLCFAs within peroxisomes.
(B) Impaired import of proteins required for peroxisome biogenesis into the peroxisomal matrix.
(C) Defective synthesis of plasmalogens, resulting in abnormal myelin structure and function.
(D) Accumulation of phytanic acid due to impaired alpha-oxidation within peroxisomes.
(E) Deficiency of mitochondrial beta-oxidation enzymes, leading to accumulation of medium-chain fatty acids.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Presentation:** 6-month-old male with lethargy, poor feeding, hypotonia, seizures, developmental delay, high-pitched cry, distinctive facial features (prominent forehead, flattened profile, epicanthal folds), hepatomegaly, jaundice, cryptorchidism, absent deep tendon reflexes. This constellation of symptoms points towards a severe congenital metabolic disorder affecting multiple organ systems, particularly the brain, liver, and development.
*   **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated liver enzymes (AST, ALT), elevated CK, and markedly elevated VLCFAs (C26:0) in plasma. Elevated VLCFAs are a hallmark of peroxisomal disorders. Elevated liver enzymes and CK suggest liver and muscle involvement, respectively.
*   **Skin Biopsy:** Abnormally small peroxisomes with an increased number. This finding is characteristic of peroxisomal biogenesis disorders, where peroxisomes fail to mature properly.
*   **Genetic Testing:** Homozygous mutation in the *PEX1* gene. *PEX1* encodes Pex1p, a peroxinsomal matrix protein receptor essential for the import of most peroxisomal proteins into the peroxisome during its biogenesis. Mutations in *PEX* genes cause Zellweger spectrum disorders.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the underlying defect. The clinical picture, lab findings (especially VLCFA elevation), biopsy results (abnormal peroxisomes), and genetic confirmation (*PEX1* mutation) strongly indicate Zellweger syndrome, a peroxisomal biogenesis disorder.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Zellweger Syndrome Pathophysiology:** This is the most severe form of peroxisomal biogenesis disorder (PBD). It results from mutations in *PEX* genes, which encode proteins (peroxins) essential for the import of newly synthesized proteins into the peroxisome during its formation. Pex1p, encoded by *PEX1*, is a receptor located in the peroxisomal membrane that recognizes and binds to specific targeting signals (PTS1) on precursor proteins in the cytosol. This complex then docks with the peroxisomal membrane, allowing the precursor protein to translocate into the peroxisomal matrix. Mutations in *PEX1* or other *PEX* genes disrupt this import process.
*   **Consequences of Defective Peroxisome Biogenesis:** Failure of peroxisome biogenesis leads to the formation of numerous, abnormally small, non-functional peroxisomes. This impairs multiple peroxisomal functions, including:
    *   Beta-oxidation of VLCFAs (very long-chain fatty acids).
    *   Synthesis of plasmalogens (ether phospholipids crucial for myelin).
    *   Alpha-oxidation of branched-chain fatty acids (like phytanic acid).
    *   Synthesis of bile acids.
    *   Detoxification of reactive oxygen species.
*   **Clinical Manifestations:** The impaired functions lead to the characteristic clinical features: VLCFA accumulation (causing neurological dysfunction, retinal dystrophy), plasmalogen deficiency (causing neurological dysfunction, cardiomyopathy), impaired bile acid synthesis (causing cholestasis, jaundice), and other systemic effects. The *PEX1* mutation specifically disrupts the import of numerous peroxisomal proteins, leading to a severe, generalized failure of peroxisome biogenesis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Deficiency of acyl-CoA oxidase, leading to impaired beta-oxidation of VLCFAs within peroxisomes.** Acyl-CoA oxidase is a key enzyme involved in the initial step of VLCFA beta-oxidation within peroxisomes. Deficiency of this enzyme causes specific peroxisomal beta-oxidation disorders, like X-linked adrenoleukodystrophy (X-ALD) or infantile Refsum disease. While these conditions also involve VLCFA accumulation, the underlying defect is an enzyme deficiency *within* a functional peroxisome, not a failure of peroxisome biogenesis itself. The skin biopsy in Zellweger syndrome shows abnormal peroxisomes, not absent or structurally normal peroxisomes with a single enzyme defect.
*   **(C) Defective synthesis of plasmalogens, resulting in abnormal myelin structure and function.** Plasmalogen synthesis is indeed impaired in Zellweger syndrome due to the lack of functional peroxisomes. However, this is a *consequence* of the underlying defect in peroxisome biogenesis, not the primary defect itself. The primary defect is the failure to form functional peroxisomes, which then leads to impaired plasmalogen synthesis and other metabolic abnormalities.
*   **(D) Accumulation of phytanic acid due to impaired alpha-oxidation within peroxisomes.** Phytanic acid accumulation occurs in Refsum disease, which is caused by a deficiency in phytanoyl-CoA hydroxylase, the enzyme responsible for alpha-oxidation of phytanic acid in peroxisomes. While Zellweger syndrome patients may have slightly elevated phytanic acid levels due to the general peroxisomal dysfunction, the hallmark biochemical finding is VLCFA elevation, and the primary defect is not alpha-oxidation itself.
*   **(E) Deficiency of mitochondrial beta-oxidation enzymes, leading to accumulation of medium-chain fatty acids.** Defects in mitochondrial beta-oxidation typically lead to the accumulation of medium-chain fatty acids (MCFAs) or long-chain fatty acids (LCFAs), depending on the specific enzyme affected (e.g., MCAD deficiency, VLCAD deficiency). These are distinct from the VLCFA accumulation seen in peroxisomal disorders like Zellweger syndrome. Furthermore, the clinical features (facial dysmorphism, specific neurological signs, abnormal peroxisomes on biopsy) are characteristic of Zellweger syndrome, not mitochondrial fatty acid oxidation defects.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature                     | Zellweger Syndrome (PBD)                                  | X-linked Adrenoleukodystrophy (X-ALD) | Refsum Disease (Classic)              | MCAD Deficiency (Mitochondrial)       |
| :-------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------ | :----------------------------------- | :------------------------------------ |
| **Primary Defect**          | Peroxisome biogenesis (PEX gene mutation)                 | Acyl-CoA oxidase deficiency           | Phytanoyl-CoA hydroxylase deficiency | MCAD enzyme deficiency               |
| **Organelle Affected**      | Peroxisome (biogenesis)                                   | Peroxisome (enzyme)                   | Peroxisome (enzyme)                  | Mitochondria                         |
| **Key Biochemical Finding** | Elevated VLCFAs (C26:0, C27:0)                            | Elevated VLCFAs                       | Elevated phytanic acid               | Elevated MCFA dicarboxylic acids      |
| **Plasmalogens**            | Decreased                                                 | Normal                                | Normal                               | Normal                                |
| **Peroxisome Morphology**   | Numerous, small, non-functional peroxisomes               | Normal number, normal size/function   | Normal number, normal size/function  | Normal                                |
| **Typical Age of Onset**    | Neonatal/Infancy (severe)                                 | Childhood/Adulthood                  | Childhood/Adulthood                  | Infancy/Early Childhood               |
| **Key Clinical Features**   | Severe hypotonia, seizures, dysmorphic features, hepatomegaly | Neurological decline, adrenal insufficiency | Retinitis pigmentosa, neuropathy     | Hypoketotic hypoglycemia, lethargy    |
| **Gene Involved**           | *PEX* genes (e.g., *PEX1*)                                | *ABCD1*                               | *PHYH*                               | *ACADM*                               |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Zellweger syndrome is a classic example of a peroxisomal biogenesis disorder caused by mutations in *PEX* genes, leading to impaired protein import and the formation of non-functional peroxisomes. This results in the accumulation of VLCFAs and deficiency of plasmalogens, causing severe neurological dysfunction and multi-system involvement. Differentiating it from other peroxisomal disorders (like X-ALD or Refsum disease) requires understanding the specific biochemical defect and the resulting peroxisome morphology. Remember that Zellweger syndrome involves a failure of peroxisome *formation*, not just a deficiency of a single enzyme within an otherwise normal peroxisome.
